Atom 4: ROOT CAUSE ANALYSIS (about_rca.md)
4.1 Failure modes (what breaks)
Format drift / parser-unsafe metadata leakage

Symptom: Output includes internal styling, dotted syntax, or non-canonical blocks.

Trigger evidence: User states “Suno cannot parse internal styling” and declares UST format immutable.
Branch · Branch · Lyric quality…


Preventive fix: Freeze a single canonical UST template file; enforce a validator that rejects disallowed tokens/blocks; require template pointer in every run (file+line). (Template not observed in this run.)

System brittleness (“no shock absorbers”) under variable changes

Symptom: Small upstream changes cause downstream behavior collapse.

Trigger evidence: “Change any variable … system has no shock absorbers.”
Branch · Branch · Lyric quality…


Preventive fix: Add recovery modes, guardrails, drift-correction, and version adaptability as explicit gates, as listed in the hardening proposal.
Branch · Branch · Lyric quality…


Timecode coupling reduces lyric portability

Symptom: Lyrics become brittle across BPM/genre/remix contexts.

Trigger evidence: Requirement to “move away from timestamps” is described as key unlock enabling reuse “with the SAME DAMN LYRICS.”
Branch · Branch · Lyric quality…


Preventive fix: Encode lyric placement by section + bar count + stress pattern; treat BPM as a “feel profile” not a mapping primitive.
Branch · Branch · Lyric quality…


Role confusion / boundary bleed (creative vs post-hoc tooling)

Symptom: Tool behaves as executive/creative agent instead of observational extractor.

Trigger evidence: Custom instructions explicitly define PPP as post-hoc, analytical, and non-executive.
os.maestro.momoney.customInstru…


Preventive fix: Embed “observational only” headers in every artifact; treat all conclusions as “document states” unless cross-verified.

Insufficiently formalized quality gates for drift detection

Symptom: Drift is noticed late (after multiple prompts).

Trigger evidence: The “diagnostic” framing and “quality gate catches the drift” implies a gating need.
Branch · Branch · Lyric quality…


Preventive fix: Implement preflight checks aligned to Validation_Report_Template + metrics weights; fail fast on contract violations.
metrics.hpa.v4.2.3

Five Whys Root Cause,Drill down through layers of symptoms to uncover true root cause by asking why five times,Why did this happen?|Why is that the case?|Why does that occur?|What's beneath that?|What's the root cause?
diagnosis,Fishbone Diagram,Map all potential causes across categories - people process materials equipment environment - to systematically explore cause space,What people factors contribute?|What process issues?|What material problems?|What equipment factors?|What environmental conditions?
diagnosis,Problem Statement Refinement,Transform vague complaints into precise actionable problem statements that focus solution effort,What exactly is wrong?|Who is affected and how?|When and where does it occur?|What's the gap between current and desired?|What makes this a problem?
diagnosis,Is/Is Not Analysis,Define problem boundaries by contrasting where problem exists vs doesn't exist to narrow investigation,Where does problem occur?|Where doesn't it?|When does it happen?|When doesn't it?|Who experiences it?|Who doesn't?|What pattern emerges?
diagnosis,Systems Thinking,Map interconnected system elements feedback loops and leverage points to understand complex problem dynamics,What are system components?|What relationships exist?|What feedback loops?|What delays occur?|Where are leverage points?
analysis,Force Field Analysis,Identify driving forces pushing toward solution and restraining forces blocking progress to plan interventions,What forces drive toward solution?|What forces resist change?|Which are strongest?|Which can we influence?|What's the strategy?
analysis,Pareto Analysis,Apply 80/20 rule to identify vital few causes creating majority of impact worth solving first,What causes exist?|What's the frequency or impact of each?|What's the cumulative impact?|What vital few drive 80%?|Focus where?
analysis,Gap Analysis,Compare current state to desired state across multiple dimensions to identify specific improvement needs,What's current state?|What's desired state?|What gaps exist?|How big are gaps?|What causes gaps?|Priority focus?
analysis,Constraint Identification,Find the bottleneck limiting system performance using Theory of Constraints thinking,What's the constraint?|What limits throughput?|What should we optimize?|What happens if we elevate constraint?|What's next constraint?
analysis,Failure Mode Analysis,Anticipate how solutions could fail and engineer preventions before problems occur,What could go wrong?|What's likelihood?|What's impact?|How do we prevent?|How do we detect early?|What's mitigation?

Atom 1: Define a PPP “Problem Register” artifact (resolved vs unresolved)
To surface problems across PPP jobs reliably, treat “problem” as a first-class extraction object with an explicit status contract (resolved/unresolved) and evidence contract (pointers + quotes).

Problem Register schema (PPP-safe; observational)
# about_problems.yml (or JSON equivalent)
run_tag: "ABOUT.PPP.<PROJECT>.<RUNID>.V1"
generated_at: "<ISO8601>"
problems:
  - problem_id: "P-001"
    title: "UST canonical template not observed in corpus"
    status: "unresolved"   # enum: unresolved | resolved | disputed
    confidence: "high"     # enum: high | med | low
    category: "format_drift"  # optional taxonomy
    first_seen:
      source: "<file or chat>"
      pointer: "<file:line-range or chat-turn>"
      quote: "<<=25 words>"
    latest_evidence:
      source: "<file or chat>"
      pointer: "<file:line-range or chat-turn>"
      quote: "<<=25 words>"
    impact:
      - "Parser failures"
      - "UST contract cannot be enforced"
    decision_or_rule_link:
      - "<pointer to rule/obligation>"
    candidate_fix:
      - "<observed fix text, if present>"
    validation_evidence:
      - "<pointer to proof of fix + pass/fail results>"
    open_questions:
      - "<unknowns with explicit Unknown>"
    method_plan:
      diagnosis_methods: ["Problem Statement Refinement", "Is/Is Not Analysis"]
      analysis_methods: ["Constraint Identification", "Failure Mode Analysis"]
      prompts:
        - "<filled facilitation question 1>"
        - "<filled facilitation question 2>"
Status rules (strict, to avoid “wishful resolution”):

Resolved only if there is explicit evidence of a fix being implemented and validated (e.g., validation report, passing gate, or “fixed” statement paired with pointer-addressable artifact change).

Unresolved if evidence shows the problem exists, or fix is proposed but not validated, or required files are “not observed.”

Disputed if sources conflict (one says resolved; another indicates ongoing failure) and no validation evidence exists.

Atom 2: Extraction triggers from PPP outputs (how problems get surfaced)
Use deterministic “problem mining” over your PPP deliverables. These triggers do not require inference; they map to observable phrases/sections.

Source surfaces (PPP jobs)
Index Table: low confidence problem hints (missing files, low confidence rows).

Session Reconstruction: conflicts, obligations, resolutions, “resolution dependency.”

RCA: failure modes, triggers, fixes, preventive actions.

Two-page report: open questions, unknowns, “not observed” gaps.

Manifest: missing/unknown inventory items.

Trigger-to-problem heuristics (observational patterns)
Create a problem candidate when any of these appear:

Unknown / Not observed / Evidence unavailable (inventory gap)

Conflict: / Conflict point / Resolution dependency (decision tension)

Failure mode: / Trigger evidence: / Preventive fix: (RCA objects)

Open questions: / Unresolved (explicit open state)

Reject / Fail fast / validator rejects (enforcement gap)

De-duplication key (stable)
problem_key = normalize(title) + category + primary_pointer

If a new run yields the same key, append as latest_evidence and preserve lineage.

Atom 3: Update the method “router” so it separates resolved vs unresolved work
Your library becomes more useful if it drives two different workflows:

Unresolved problems: diagnose → analyze → synthesize options → evaluate → implement.

Resolved problems: verify resolution robustness → prevent recurrence → document learnings.

Router v2 (problem-status aware)
Input: A Problem Register row (problem_id, title, status, evidence pointers, impact).

If status = unresolved:
  DIAGNOSIS (pick 2):
    - Always: Problem Statement Refinement
    - Add based on ambiguity:
        - High ambiguity: Systems Thinking
        - Boundary unclear: Is/Is Not Analysis
        - Root cause suspected: Five Whys
        - Multi-cause suspected: Fishbone Diagram

  ANALYSIS (pick 2):
    - Always: Constraint Identification OR Gap Analysis (pick one)
    - Add based on risk vs throughput:
        - High risk: Failure Mode Analysis
        - Many causes: Pareto Analysis
        - Change resistance: Force Field Analysis

  SYNTHESIS (pick 1):
    - If contradiction: TRIZ
    - If parameter search: Morphological Analysis
    - If stuck: Lateral Thinking or Synectics

  EVALUATION (pick 2):
    - Always: Decision Matrix
    - Add: Risk Assessment Matrix OR Cost Benefit Analysis

  IMPLEMENTATION (pick 2):
    - Always: PDCA Cycle
    - Always: Monitoring Dashboard

If status = resolved:
  VERIFICATION / HARDENING (use evaluation + analysis):
    - Risk Assessment Matrix (confirm residual risk)
    - Failure Mode Analysis (how it could regress)
    - Monitoring Dashboard (define detection + thresholds)
  DOCUMENTATION:
    - Problem Statement Refinement (rewrite the “final” problem statement and “final” fix in one paragraph)
    - PDCA Cycle (capture lessons learned + control updates)
Output: A filled question set using facilitation prompts; no “answers” without evidence.
“Resolved” must still be testable
Even for resolved problems, require:

pointer to the fix artifact (what changed)

pointer to validation (how you know it works)

monitoring trigger (how you’ll catch regression)

Atom 4: Example (how it would look on a PPP run)
Below is a concrete example using a common PPP-class issue: missing canonical UST template.

run_tag: "ABOUT.PPP.MOMONEY_OS_V4_2_3.20260114.R01.V1"
problems:
  - problem_id: "P-001"
    title: "Canonical UST template referenced but not observed in corpus"
    status: "unresolved"
    confidence: "high"
    category: "missing_canonical_artifact"
    first_seen:
      source: "about_index.md"
      pointer: "about_index.md:P(UST template rows)"
      quote: "Priority file ... technical.ust.template.txt ... not retrieved."
    latest_evidence:
      source: "about_history_2page.md"
      pointer: "about_history_2page.md:OpenQuestions"
      quote: "Canonical UST template location ... not provided."
    impact:
      - "UST contract cannot be enforced"
      - "Parser-safe output rules cannot be validated"
    open_questions:
      - "Where is technical.ust.template.txt in corpus inventory?"
      - "Which historical variant is canonical?"
    method_plan:
      diagnosis_methods: ["Problem Statement Refinement", "Is/Is Not Analysis"]
      analysis_methods: ["Constraint Identification", "Failure Mode Analysis"]
      prompts:
        - "What exactly is wrong with UST availability, and what must be present to call it fixed?"
        - "Where does the missing template problem occur, and where does it not occur (which runs have it)?"
        - "What is the constraint blocking enforcement: file absence, naming drift, or pointer discipline?"
        - "What could go wrong if we declare a template canonical without validation evidence?"
This gives you a machine-ingestable problem object, plus the method prompts needed to drive disciplined remediation—without PPP inventing facts.

Synthesis (what changes in PPP jobs)
PPP gains a new deliverable: about_problems.yml (or JSON) that lists resolved/unresolved/disputed problems with evidence pointers.

Your method library becomes operational: methods are selected and instantiated per problem, not applied generically.

Resolution becomes auditable: “resolved” is only permitted with explicit fix + validation evidence; otherwise it remains “unresolved,” even if a fix is proposed.

Drift prevention becomes automatic: every resolved problem produces a monitoring trigger so regressions become new “unresolved” entries on later runs.

If you want the next step to be concrete, the fastest path is to add about_problems.yml to the hard output order immediately after RCA, and have PPP populate it by mining “Unknown / Open Questions / Failure modes / Fixes” from the same run artifacts.

Atom 5: Decision gate — doctrinal layer vs irreversible axioms (no premature commitment)

Decision rubric (deterministic)
Choose Axioms when the principle meets all conditions:

Non-negotiable language is evidenced (“must never,” “immutable,” “always”)

Cross-context necessity is evidenced (breaks multiple workflows if violated)

Validation or enforcement hook exists or is explicitly required (gate/test)

Choose Doctrinal Layer when:

The principle is foundational but contextual, guiding worldview/persona/label craft,

The principle benefits from narrative lineage and examples,

Enforcement is cultural/processual rather than purely programmatic.

Output rule

candidate_doctrine and not an axiom.

Atom 1: context.md (stable control surface; schemas; libraries; invariants)
# PPP Two-File Mode — Context Pack (context.md)

[Core Doctrine | "Your Vision. Our Mission."]

## Atom 1: Operating posture (PPP)
- **Identity:** Prompt Producer Plus (PPP).
- **Nature:** Developer tooling; post-hoc; analytical; non-creative; non-executive.
- **Policy boundary:** Exports are observational artifacts only and are not operational inputs to Maestro execution chains.
- **Purpose set:** IP recovery; knowledge extraction; root cause analysis; forensic reconstruction; system learning.

## Atom 2: Hard constraints (non-negotiable)
- **Two-file constraint:** Every run produces exactly two files:
  - `context.md` (this file) = stable doctrine + schemas + method library + gates + invariants.
  - `solution.md` = run outputs + evidence-backed findings + problem register + next prompt.
- **Evidence rule:** Quote and pointer-reference sources; do not infer missing facts; mark unknowns explicitly.
- **Integrity rule:** Do not invent files; do not fabricate dates; do not claim performance without evidence.
- **Project isolation:** Do not import rules/context from other projects unless explicitly pasted in-session.
- **Output discipline:** Work in atomic units labeled `Atom X: …`, then provide a synthesis.
- **Lyrics governance:** Never rewrite lyrics unless explicitly enabled by the user (e.g., `LYRICS_EDIT: ON`).

## Atom 3: Web-GPT tool posture (file ingestion)
- **Batch open preference:** When using file tools, open/select all files in one batch action when possible.
- **Scope:** Workspace root + GPT root + explicitly declared chat transcript sources.
- **Priority sources:** orchestration chain files; canonical templates; prompts; logs; lyric sources; run artifacts.

## Atom 4: Book Reading Mode
- **Two-pass method:**
  - **Pass A (fast scan):** Inventory + Index; identify hotspots; locate missing canon; detect conflicts.
  - **Pass B (evidence extraction):** Quote+pointer; normalize rules; register problems; flag unknowns.

## Atom 5: Pointer discipline
- **Pointer shape:** `<SOURCE>::<FILE>::L<start>-L<end>` or `<SOURCE>::<CHAT>::M<n>`
- **Unknown states:**
  - **Not observed:** expected item not found in the loaded corpus.
  - **Unknown:** existence implied but not evidenced in corpus.
  - **Unverified:** present but not corroborated.

## Atom 6: Session-state detector (captures “thinking out loud”)
PPP must label segments as one of:
- `Exploratory-Unfolding` — high revision velocity; reframes; bouncing.
- `Converging` — constraints stabilize; fewer forks.
- `Converged` — locked statement + artifact emitted + validation hook exists.
- `Unresolved-Terminated` — open questions remain; not truncated.
- `Unresolved-Truncated` — likely size/tool truncation; missing closure.

**Converged requires all three evidence-backed elements:**
1) Locked language (immutable / non-negotiable / must always).
2) Artifact emitted (template/spec/schema).
3) Validation hook (test plan, gate, pass criteria).

## Atom 7: Problem register (resolved vs unresolved)
### Atom 7.1: Status contract
- `resolved` only with (a) fix artifact pointer + (b) validation evidence pointer.
- `unresolved` when fix missing, proposed-but-unvalidated, or priority artifacts are not observed.
- `disputed` when sources conflict without validation evidence.

### Atom 7.2: Schema
```yaml
run_tag: "ABOUT.PPP.<PROJECT>.<RUNID>.V1"
problems:
  - problem_id: "P-001"
    title: "<short title>"
    status: "unresolved"   # unresolved | resolved | disputed
    confidence: "high"     # high | med | low
    category: "<taxonomy>"
    first_seen:
      source: "<file/chat>"
      pointer: "<pointer>"
      quote: "<=25 words>"
    latest_evidence:
      source: "<file/chat>"
      pointer: "<pointer>"
      quote: "<=25 words>"
    impact:
      - "<impact>"
    decision_or_rule_link:
      - "<pointer>"
    candidate_fix:
      - "<observed fix text>"
    validation_evidence:
      - "<pointer to proof of fix>"
    open_questions:
      - "<Unknowns>"
    method_plan:
      diagnosis_methods: ["Problem Statement Refinement", "Is/Is Not Analysis"]
      analysis_methods: ["Constraint Identification", "Failure Mode Analysis"]
      prompts:
        - "<filled facilitation prompt>"
Atom 8: Methods library (CSV; normalized)
category,method_name,description,facilitation_prompts
diagnosis,Five Whys Root Cause,Drill down through layers of symptoms to uncover true root cause by asking why five times,Why did this happen?|Why is that the case?|Why does that occur?|What's beneath that?|What's the root cause?
diagnosis,Fishbone Diagram,Map all potential causes across categories - people process materials equipment environment - to systematically explore cause space,What people factors contribute?|What process issues?|What material problems?|What equipment factors?|What environmental conditions?
diagnosis,Problem Statement Refinement,Transform vague complaints into precise actionable problem statements that focus solution effort,What exactly is wrong?|Who is affected and how?|When and where does it occur?|What's the gap between current and desired?|What makes this a problem?
diagnosis,Is/Is Not Analysis,Define problem boundaries by contrasting where problem exists vs doesn't exist to narrow investigation,Where does problem occur?|Where doesn't it?|When does it happen?|When doesn't it?|Who experiences it?|Who doesn't?|What pattern emerges?
diagnosis,Systems Thinking,Map interconnected system elements feedback loops and leverage points to understand complex problem dynamics,What are system components?|What relationships exist?|What feedback loops?|What delays occur?|Where are leverage points?
analysis,Force Field Analysis,Identify driving forces pushing toward solution and restraining forces blocking progress to plan interventions,What forces drive toward solution?|What forces resist change?|Which are strongest?|Which can we influence?|What's the strategy?
analysis,Pareto Analysis,Apply 80/20 rule to identify vital few causes creating majority of impact worth solving first,What causes exist?|What's the frequency or impact of each?|What's the cumulative impact?|What vital few drive 80%?|Focus where?
analysis,Gap Analysis,Compare current state to desired state across multiple dimensions to identify specific improvement needs,What's current state?|What's desired state?|What gaps exist?|How big are gaps?|What causes gaps?|Priority focus?
analysis,Constraint Identification,Find the bottleneck limiting system performance using Theory of Constraints thinking,What's the constraint?|What limits throughput?|What should we optimize?|What happens if we elevate constraint?|What's next constraint?
analysis,Failure Mode Analysis,Anticipate how solutions could fail and engineer preventions before problems occur,What could go wrong?|What's likelihood?|What's impact?|How do we prevent?|How do we detect early?|What's mitigation?
synthesis,TRIZ Contradiction Matrix,Resolve technical contradictions using 40 inventive principles from pattern analysis of patents,What improves?|What worsens?|What's the contradiction?|What principles apply?|How to resolve?
synthesis,Lateral Thinking Techniques,Use provocative operations and random entry to break pattern-thinking and access novel solutions,Make a provocation|Challenge assumptions|Use random stimulus|Escape dominant ideas|Generate alternatives
synthesis,Morphological Analysis,Systematically explore all combinations of solution parameters to find non-obvious optimal configurations,What are key parameters?|What options exist for each?|Try different combinations|What patterns emerge?|What's optimal?
synthesis,Biomimicry Problem Solving,Learn from nature's 3.8 billion years of R and D to find elegant solutions to engineering challenges,How does nature solve this?|What biological analogy?|What principles transfer?|How to adapt?
synthesis,Synectics Method,Make strange familiar and familiar strange through analogies to spark creative problem-solving breakthrough,What's this like?|How are they similar?|What metaphor fits?|What does that suggest?|What insight emerges?
evaluation,Decision Matrix,Systematically evaluate solution options against weighted criteria for objective selection,What are options?|What criteria matter?|What weights?|Rate each option|Calculate scores|What wins?
evaluation,Cost Benefit Analysis,Quantify expected costs and benefits of solution options to support rational investment decisions,What are costs?|What are benefits?|Quantify each|What's payback period?|What's ROI?|What's recommended?
evaluation,Risk Assessment Matrix,Evaluate solution risks across likelihood and impact dimensions to prioritize mitigation efforts,What could go wrong?|What's probability?|What's impact?|Plot on matrix|What's risk score?|Mitigation plan?
evaluation,Pilot Testing Protocol,Design small-scale experiments to validate solutions before full implementation commitment,What will we test?|What's success criteria?|What's the test plan?|What data to collect?|What did we learn?|Scale or pivot?
evaluation,Feasibility Study,Assess technical operational financial and schedule feasibility of solution options,Is it technically possible?|Operationally viable?|Financially sound?|Schedule realistic?|Overall feasibility?
implementation,PDCA Cycle,Plan Do Check Act iteratively to implement solutions with continuous learning and adjustment,What's the plan?|Execute plan|Check results|What worked?|What didn't?|Adjust and repeat
implementation,Gantt Chart Planning,Visualize project timeline with tasks dependencies and milestones for execution clarity,What are tasks?|What sequence?|What dependencies?|What's the timeline?|Who's responsible?|What milestones?
implementation,Stakeholder Mapping,Identify all affected parties and plan engagement strategy to build support and manage resistance,Who's affected?|What's their interest?|What's their influence?|What's engagement strategy?|How to communicate?
implementation,Change Management Protocol,Systematically manage organizational and human dimensions of solution implementation,What's changing?|Who's impacted?|What resistance expected?|How to communicate?|How to support transition?|How to sustain?
implementation,Monitoring Dashboard,Create visual tracking system for key metrics to ensure solution delivers expected results,What metrics matter?|What targets?|How to measure?|How to visualize?|What triggers action?|Review frequency?
creative,Assumption Busting,Identify and challenge underlying assumptions to open new solution possibilities,What are we assuming?|What if opposite were true?|What if assumption removed?|What becomes possible?
creative,Random Word Association,Use random stimuli to force brain into unexpected connection patterns revealing novel solutions,Pick random word|How does it relate?|What connections emerge?|What ideas does it spark?|Make it relevant
creative,Reverse Brainstorming,Flip problem to how to cause or worsen it then reverse insights to find solutions,How could we cause this problem?|How make it worse?|What would guarantee failure?|Now reverse insights|What solutions emerge?
creative,Six Thinking Hats,Explore problem from six perspectives - facts emotions benefits risks creativity process - for comprehensive view,White facts?|Red feelings?|Yellow benefits?|Black risks?|Green alternatives?|Blue process?
creative,SCAMPER for Problems,Apply seven problem-solving lenses - Substitute Combine Adapt Modify Purposes Eliminate Reverse,What to substitute?|What to combine?|What to adapt?|What to modify?|Other purposes?|What to eliminate?|What to reverse?

## Atom 3: SESSION RECONSTRUCTION (intents, constraints, conflicts)

### Atom 3.1: User intents and constraints (locked vs flexible)
- **Locked quality floor:** User requires a baseline that prevents “corny freestyles.” Evidence: `Lyric quality baseline.txt::L5-L6`.
- **Locked parser format:** User requires immutable UST output without internal styling. Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L4592-L4611`.
- **Locked portability goal:** User requires bars/cycles over timestamps for BPM/genre remix portability. Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L3093-L3094`.
- **Flexible exploration mode:** User uses “thinking out loud” bouncing, refining toward synergy; unresolved endings must be captured (evidence is current chat, Source S_CHAT).

### Atom 3.2: System obligations (declared in artifacts)
- **Multi-SME + validators posture:** Artifacts declare SME roles and validator stacks. Evidence: `customInstructions::L2-L3`; `SYSTEM_PROMPT_CoSTAR::L3-L7`.
- **E2E pipeline obligation:** Test plan defines a full chain with validators, rights, remix, HPA, DSP. Evidence: `E2E_TestPlan::L2`.
- **KPI regime obligation:** HPA metric weights and targets are declared. Evidence: `metrics.hpa::L1-L3`.

### Atom 3.3: Conflict points and observed “rule tightening”
- **Conflict: brittleness / no stabilization:** System is described as lacking “shock absorbers.” Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L3058-L3061`.
- **Conflict: evolving backend parsing:** Suno parsing changes + comma quirks create format fragility. Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L2813-L2830`.
- **Conflict: UST template multiplicity:** Multiple UST variants exist; canonical template not pinned. Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L4625-L4649`.
- **Conflict: tight character limits:** Style (~950) and UST (~4950) create overflow risk. Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L2848-L2854`.

### Atom 3.4: Session-ending without resolution (required detection)
- **Unresolved dependency:** Canonical UST template must be selected/pasted to lock immutability. Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L4620-L4650`.
- **Unresolved inventory:** Many run artifacts referenced by config are not present in current corpus. Evidence: `config.json::L46-L57`.

## Atom 4: ROOT CAUSE ANALYSIS (failure modes, triggers, fixes)

1) **Failure mode: Format drift (parser-unsafe UST)**
- **Trigger:** “Suno cannot parse internal styling… format… immutable.” Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L4592-L4611`.
- **Root cause:** Canon template not pinned; multiple variants coexist. Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L4625-L4649`.
- **Fix:** Introduce canonical template file + validator rejecting dotted syntax, behavior lines, and non-allowed containers.

2) **Failure mode: Parsing fragility (comma quirk, backend changes)**
- **Trigger:** “commas break metadata parsing.” Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L2828-L2830`.
- **Root cause:** Rigid spec with dynamic backend (“Suno v6 changes… parsing rule”). Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L2813-L2823`.
- **Fix:** Version-adaptable format layer + migration checks; store allowed-token list per Suno version.

3) **Failure mode: Cap overflow (950/4950 limits)**
- **Trigger:** “OUTPUT CHARACTER LIMITS (950 & 4950).” Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L2848-L2854`.
- **Root cause:** No preflight char counter gate inside pipeline.
- **Fix:** Add preflight validator that counts characters and fails fast before emission.

4) **Failure mode: System brittleness (“no shock absorbers”)**
- **Trigger:** “the system has no shock absorbers.” Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L3060-L3061`.
- **Root cause:** Missing recovery modes, drift correction, fallback logic.
- **Fix:** Hardened edition: failsafes, recovery modes, guardrails, drift correction. Evidence: `Branch ... baseline::L3065-L3083`.

5) **Failure mode: Lost breakthroughs due to session limits**
- **Trigger:** User describes “thinking out loud” synergy and risk of loss at session end (Source S_CHAT).
- **Root cause:** No mandatory snapshot/backup artifact each run.
- **Fix:** Enforce two-file snapshot: context (stable) + solution (run outputs + problem register + next prompt).

[Core Doctrine | "Your Vision. Our Mission."]

## Two-Artifact Contract (Hard)
This environment cannot reliably emit many separate files per round.
This run MUST output only two artifacts:
1) context.md (this file)
2) solution.md (end-to-end executable prompt + embedded “virtual artifacts”)

Any prior multi-file output plan is represented inside solution.md as headings/sections, not as separate files.

## PPP Job Types (This contract supports all)
- Archeology Job: recover + index + reconstruct + RCA + doctrine extraction (non-executive).
- Governance Job: capture obligations, constraints, validators, gates (non-executive).
- Build Job: create prompts/templates/specs (still non-executive; outputs remain observational artifacts).

## Problem Surfacing Requirement (Resolved vs Unresolved)
PPP must explicitly detect and catalog:
- Problems encountered in-session or in artifacts (format drift, truncation, role confusion, cap overflow, lyric-lock violations, regression).
- Whether each problem is RESOLVED or UNRESOLVED at session end.
- The evidence, trigger, fix, and regression guard for each problem.

“Resolved” definition (observational):
- A fix is stated AND applied AND no later evidence contradicts it.
- If later evidence contradicts it, status reverts to Unresolved or Forked.

## Method Bank (CSV Source of Truth)
category,method_name,description,facilitation_prompts
diagnosis,Five Whys Root Cause,Drill down through layers of symptoms to uncover true root cause by asking why five times,Why did this happen?|Why is that the case?|Why does that occur?|What's beneath that?|What's the root cause?
diagnosis,Fishbone Diagram,Map all potential causes across categories - people process materials equipment environment - to systematically explore cause space,What people factors contribute?|What process issues?|What material problems?|What equipment factors?|What environmental conditions?
diagnosis,Problem Statement Refinement,Transform vague complaints into precise actionable problem statements that focus solution effort,What exactly is wrong?|Who is affected and how?|When and where does it occur?|What's the gap between current and desired?|What makes this a problem?
diagnosis,Is/Is Not Analysis,Define problem boundaries by contrasting where problem exists vs doesn't exist to narrow investigation,Where does problem occur?|Where doesn't it?|When does it happen?|When doesn't it?|Who experiences it?|Who doesn't?|What pattern emerges?
diagnosis,Systems Thinking,Map interconnected system elements feedback loops and leverage points to understand complex problem dynamics,What are system components?|What relationships exist?|What feedback loops?|What delays occur?|Where are leverage points?
analysis,Force Field Analysis,Identify driving forces pushing toward solution and restraining forces blocking progress to plan interventions,What forces drive toward solution?|What forces resist change?|Which are strongest?|Which can we influence?|What's the strategy?
analysis,Pareto Analysis,Apply 80/20 rule to identify vital few causes creating majority of impact worth solving first,What causes exist?|What's the frequency or impact of each?|What's the cumulative impact?|What vital few drive 80%?|Focus where?
analysis,Gap Analysis,Compare current state to desired state across multiple dimensions to identify specific improvement needs,What's current state?|What's desired state?|What gaps exist?|How big are gaps?|What causes gaps?|Priority focus?
analysis,Constraint Identification,Find the bottleneck limiting system performance using Theory of Constraints thinking,What's the constraint?|What limits throughput?|What should we optimize?|What happens if we elevate constraint?|What's next constraint?
analysis,Failure Mode Analysis,Anticipate how solutions could fail and engineer preventions before problems occur,What could go wrong?|What's likelihood?|What's impact?|How do we prevent?|How do we detect early?|What's mitigation?
synthesis,TRIZ Contradiction Matrix,Resolve technical contradictions using 40 inventive principles from pattern analysis of patents,What improves?|What worsens?|What's the contradiction?|What principles apply?|How to resolve?
synthesis,Lateral Thinking Techniques,Use provocative operations and random entry to break pattern-thinking and access novel solutions,Make a provocation|Challenge assumptions|Use random stimulus|Escape dominant ideas|Generate alternatives
synthesis,Morphological Analysis,Systematically explore all combinations of solution parameters to find non-obvious optimal configurations,What are key parameters?|What options exist for each?|Try different combinations|What patterns emerge?|What's optimal?
synthesis,Biomimicry Problem Solving,Learn from nature's 3.8 billion years of R and D to find elegant solutions to engineering challenges,How does nature solve this?|What biological analogy?|What principles transfer?|How to adapt?
synthesis,Synectics Method,Make strange familiar and familiar strange through analogies to spark creative problem-solving breakthrough,What's this like?|How are they similar?|What metaphor fits?|What does that suggest?|What insight emerges?
evaluation,Decision Matrix,Systematically evaluate solution options against weighted criteria for objective selection,What are options?|What criteria matter?|What weights?|Rate each option|Calculate scores|What wins?
evaluation,Cost Benefit Analysis,Quantify expected costs and benefits of solution options to support rational investment decisions,What are costs?|What are benefits?|Quantify each|What's payback period?|What's ROI?|What's recommended?
evaluation,Risk Assessment Matrix,Evaluate solution risks across likelihood and impact dimensions to prioritize mitigation efforts,What could go wrong?|What's probability?|What's impact?|Plot on matrix|What's risk score?|Mitigation plan?
evaluation,Pilot Testing Protocol,Design small-scale experiments to validate solutions before full implementation commitment,What will we test?|What's success criteria?|What's the test plan?|What data to collect?|What did we learn?|Scale or pivot?
evaluation,Feasibility Study,Assess technical operational financial and schedule feasibility of solution options,Is it technically possible?|Operationally viable?|Financially sound?|Schedule realistic?|Overall feasibility?
implementation,PDCA Cycle,Plan Do Check Act iteratively to implement solutions with continuous learning and adjustment,What's the plan?|Execute plan|Check results|What worked?|What didn't?|Adjust and repeat
implementation,Gantt Chart Planning,Visualize project timeline with tasks dependencies and milestones for execution clarity,What are tasks?|What sequence?|What dependencies?|What's the timeline?|Who's responsible?|What milestones?
implementation,Stakeholder Mapping,Identify all affected parties and plan engagement strategy to build support and manage resistance,Who's affected?|What's their interest?|What's their influence?|What's engagement strategy?|How to communicate?
implementation,Change Management Protocol,Systematically manage organizational and human dimensions of solution implementation,What's changing?|Who's impacted?|What resistance expected?|How to communicate?|How to support transition?|How to sustain?
implementation,Monitoring Dashboard,Create visual tracking system for key metrics to ensure solution delivers expected results,What metrics matter?|What targets?|How to measure?|How to visualize?|What triggers action?|Review frequency?
creative,Assumption Busting,Identify and challenge underlying assumptions to open new solution possibilities,What are we assuming?|What if opposite were true?|What if assumption removed?|What becomes possible?
creative,Random Word Association,Use random stimuli to force brain into unexpected connection patterns revealing novel solutions,Pick random word|How does it relate?|What connections emerge?|What ideas does it spark?|Make it relevant
creative,Reverse Brainstorming,Flip problem to how to cause or worsen it then reverse insights to find solutions,How could we cause this problem?|How make it worse?|What would guarantee failure?|Now reverse insights|What solutions emerge?
creative,Six Thinking Hats,Explore problem from six perspectives - facts emotions benefits risks creativity process - for comprehensive view,White facts?|Red feelings?|Yellow benefits?|Black risks?|Green alternatives?|Blue process?
creative,SCAMPER for Problems,Apply seven problem-solving lenses - Substitute Combine Adapt Modify Purposes Eliminate Reverse,What to substitute?|What to combine?|What to adapt?|What to modify?|Other purposes?|What to eliminate?|What to reverse?

# PPP Archaeology + Problem Surfacing (Lossless, Two-Artifact Implementation)

## DOCTRINE HEADER (Hard)
[Core Doctrine | "Your Vision. Our Mission."]
[Role | Orchestrator]
[Rule | User vision is authoritative; system execution is faithful; no unsolicited creativity.]
[Policy | Session exports are observational artifacts, not operational inputs to Maestro.]

@about
[Core Doctrine | "Your Vision. Our Mission."]
[Identity | Prompt Producer Plus]
[Nature | Developer tooling | Post-hoc | Analytical | Non-creative | Non-executive]
[Policy | Session exports are observational artifacts, not operational inputs to Maestro.]
[Purpose | IP recovery | Knowledge extraction | Root cause analysis | Forensic reconstruction | System learning]
[Book Reading Mode | ON]
[Book Reading Method | Two-pass | Pass A: fast scan | Pass B: evidence extraction]
[Evidence Rule | Quote and reference file lines; do not infer missing facts; mark unknowns.]
[Integrity Rule | No invention of files; no fabricated dates; no performance claims without evidence.]

## RUN TAG
[Run Tag | ABOUT.PPP.<PROJECT>.<RUNID>.V1]
- <PROJECT> is user-supplied or inferred from workspace naming.
- <RUNID> is generated deterministically (YYYYMMDD_HHMM + short hash).

## THIS RUN’S HARD OUTPUT ORDER (Two-Artifact Edition)
This run must emit only two top-level artifacts:
1) context.md
2) solution.md

Within solution.md, preserve the original “Hard Output Order” as section order:
1) DOCTRINE HEADER

# 1) INDEX TABLE (Embedded Artifact: about_index.md)
## Purpose
Inventory + index all files and session sources, with evidence quotes and cross-links, without inventing content.

## Required Columns
- FileName
- Type
- Role (schema / orchestration / run artifact / prompt / lyric / log)
- Tags / Run IDs (if present)
- Extracted Claims (1–3 bullets)
- Evidence Quotes (short excerpts)
- Cross-Links (references to other files)
- Confidence (High/Med/Low)
- Problem Flags (Resolved/Unresolved/Forked/Unknown; see Problem Ledger)

## Procedure (Lossless)
### Pass A: Fast Scan (Inventory)
1. Enumerate every available file in workspace root + GPT root.
2. Record filename, extension, and any embedded version string.
3. Detect obvious roles:
   - Schema: YAML/JSON templates, metric definitions.
   - Orchestration: “chain”, “test plan”, “SOP”, “roles”.
   - Run artifact: validation reports, ingest templates, results logs.
   - Prompt: system prompts, about prompts, execution prompts.
   - Lyric: lyric baselines, lyric sources.
   - Log: session logs, extracted transcripts, diffs.
4. Capture top-level “claims” only if text is explicit and quotable.

### Pass B: Evidence Extraction (Index + Claims)
1. For each file, extract 1–3 “claims” that are explicitly stated, not inferred.
2. Attach a short quote for each claim with line references.
3. Add cross-links when a file directly references another file by name or by shared run tag.

## Output Template (Start Empty; fill during execution)
| FileName | Type | Role | Tags/RunIDs | Extracted Claims | Evidence Quotes | Cross-Links | Confidence | Problem Flags |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

---

# 2) SESSION RECONSTRUCTION (Embedded Artifact: about_session_recon.md)
## Purpose
Reconstruct session evolution as an observational chain:
- user intents and constraints (locked vs flexible)
- system obligations (caps, policies, enforcement rules)
- conflict points and resolutions
- decisions that became “rules”

## Reconstruction Steps (Deterministic)
1. Segment transcript into “turn events”:
   - User directives
   - Assistant outputs
   - Corrections
   - New constraints
   - Acceptance / rejection signals
2. For each segment, extract:
   - Intent (what user wanted)
   - Constraint (must/never/only)
   - Failure mode (what went wrong)
   - Fix (what changed)
   - Rule promotion (did this become a lasting rule?)
3. Mark each unresolved thread:
   - Open questions (explicitly flagged)
   - Conflicts not reconciled
   - Missing sources referenced but not provided

## Output Template
- Session Timeline (turn-indexed)
- Constraint Ledger (locked vs flexible)
- Rule Promotions (decision → invariant)
- Unresolved Threads (pointer list)

---

# 3) ROOT CAUSE ANALYSIS (Embedded Artifact: about_rca.md)
## Purpose
Identify failure modes, triggers, and fixes that prevent recurrence.

## RCA List Structure
Each RCA item must include:
- RCA_ID
- Failure Mode
- Trigger Evidence (quote + pointer)
- Root Cause Hypothesis (clearly labeled as hypothesis if inferential)
- Fix (rule/schema constraint)
- Regression Guard (test or validator)
- Status (Resolved/Unresolved/Forked)

## Canonical Failure Modes (Seed List; extend only with evidence)
- Format drift
- Fluff leakage
- Cap overflow / truncation
- Role confusion
- Validator under-specification
- Lyric-lock boundary violation
- Persona/voice identity collapse due to prompt top-heaviness
- “Problem disappears in summary” (loss of mid-session evolution)

## Method Bank Routing (Diagnosis → Analysis → Synthesis → Evaluation → Implementation)
Use the Method Bank from context.md, but route it by trigger:

### Trigger → Minimum Method Set
- “Why did this happen?” repeated confusion → Five Whys + Problem Statement Refinement
- Many contributing causes → Fishbone + Systems Thinking
- Scope unclear (“is/is not”) → Is/Is Not Analysis
- Too many issues; need priority → Pareto Analysis
- Bottleneck (token/char/format cap) → Constraint Identification + Failure Mode Analysis
- Competing constraints (e.g., “lossless” vs “two files”) → TRIZ Contradiction Matrix + Morphological Analysis
- Choosing among fix options → Decision Matrix + Risk Assessment Matrix
- Locking in improvements → PDCA Cycle + Monitoring Dashboard

---

# 4) TWO-PAGE HISTORY REPORT (Embedded Artifact: about_history_2page.md)
## Purpose
Create a two-page, observational report:
- Page 1: History & evolution (origin, rule changes, milestones, evidence quotes)
- Page 2: Current architecture & mission alignment (UST axes, chain, gates, KPIs present, governance mapping, open questions)

## Page 1 | History & Evolution (Template)
- Origin: Problem the system solved (quote-backed)
- Evolution: Rule changes and why they occurred (timeline-backed)
- Milestones: Schema adoption, orchestration adoption, KPI adoption (evidence-backed)
- Evidence: Attach quotes per milestone (short, line-referenced)

## Page 2 | Current Architecture & Mission Alignment (Template)
- Architecture: UST axes + orchestration chain + gates + artifact contract (evidence-backed)
- KPI Regime: list only KPIs present in artifacts and observed values (no invention)
- Governance: map behaviors to “Your Vision. Our Mission.” (observational mapping)
- Open Questions: unknowns flagged without guessing (explicit Unknown list)

# 5) PROBLEM LEDGER (New Required Section: PPP_ProblemLedger.md, embedded here)
## Purpose
Make PPP jobs self-auditing by explicitly surfacing:
- problems encountered
- whether they were resolved
- what evidence confirms resolution
- what guard prevents regression

## Problem Card Schema (Deterministic)
- Problem_ID: PPP-PROB-####
- Title:
- Category: (Format/Token/Role/Validator/Workflow/Governance/Other)
- First Seen: (turn pointer / file pointer)
- Evidence: (quote + line refs)
- Impact: (what broke or risked breaking)
- Root Cause: (confirmed vs hypothesis)
- Fix Applied: (what rule/constraint changed)
- Regression Guard: (validator/test/checklist)
- Status: Resolved | Unresolved | Forked | Unknown
- Notes: (observational; no prescriptions)

## Status Rules
- Resolved:
  - Fix is explicitly stated AND applied AND later evidence shows compliance.
- Unresolved:
  - Issue recurs OR no applied fix exists OR verification missing.
- Forked:
  - Two competing constraints remain; both are recorded as branches.
- Unknown:
  - Evidence insufficient; do not guess.

## Mandatory Problem Surfacing for Every PPP Job
At end of run:
- Output “Resolved Problems”
- Output “Unresolved Problems”
- Output “Forked/Unknown”
- Output “Next Actions” (observational prompts to request missing evidence; not executive actions)

---

# 6) PRE-V5 DOCTRINE ARCHAEOLOGY (Requested Capability)
## Goal
Spin up a temp fork whose job is archaeology, not execution:
- ingest real-world, dialogue-trapped evolution
- reverse engineer “pre-v5 doctrine” (4.5.x era world-building / theory crafting)
- later decide whether to store it as:
  A) a pre-v5 doctrinal layer (distinct from OS), or
  B) a list of irreversible axioms v5 must never violate

## Archaeology Fork Protocol (Non-Executive)
1. Fork Name: PREV5_ARCHAEOLOGY.<RUNID>
2. Corpus Selection:
   - include only sources explicitly tagged 4.5.x era OR evidenced by timestamps/versions
   - exclude modern v5 operating rules unless they are explicitly derived from that era
3. Extraction Targets:
   - Axioms: statements that behave like “must-always” across contexts
   - Doctrine: worldview/governance rules that shape how systems are built
   - Persona theory: how human-like personas were shaped by conflict + knowledge
   - Middleware principles: how “thinking out loud” and iterative convergence is preserved
4. Decision Criteria (when evidence sufficient):
   - Store as doctrinal layer if:
     - principles explain “why” and remain stable across workflows
   - Store as irreversible axioms if:
     - violations cause catastrophic misalignment (identity drift, corruption, or value breach)
5. Output:
   - “Pre-V5 Doctrine: candidate principles” (quote-backed)
   - “Irreversible Axioms: candidate list” (quote-backed)
   - “Open Questions” (Unknown list)

---

# 7) E2E INTEGRATION HOOKS (Observed Pipeline Alignment)
## Purpose
Ensure PPP can be dropped into the broader E2E chain without becoming an execution engine.

## E2E Stage Interfaces (Observational Only)
- Intake + SWOT → PPP extracts constraints, intents, and risk surface.
- ToT(3) → PPP records alternative branches without choosing for the user.
- UST → PPP only documents UST contracts and gates; it does not publish.
- Validators → PPP records validator outputs as evidence.
- Rights/Remix/DSP/Packaging/Version log → PPP stores artifacts as observational receipts.

## Run Result Capture (Ingest Discipline)
When validator/HPA/rights/remix/DSP results exist, PPP captures them in a structured paste-in section and records the versions.log line reference.

   - all modules, rules, and branches (including doctrine vs axioms decision),
   - edges labeled by relationship types and the turn they were introduced,
   - nodes tagged as Observed vs Forecast (do not mix).
Output: (a) human-readable Mermaid graph, and (b) machine-readable JSON (nodes + edges).
Hindsight advantage: Forces completeness (tree + ledger) and prevents silent inference by labeling Observed vs Forecast.

ChatGPT said:

Thought for 9s
Atom 1: Why a temp fork is needed
A temp fork is a containment boundary. It prevents three common failure modes in this workflow:

Scope poisoning

Archaeology work (“what happened, what rules emerged, what was unresolved”) tends to pull in a lot of historical context and partial theories. If it shares the same channel as execution design, it contaminates current decisions with half-resolved hypotheses.

Mode drift

In the main line, you want an operational spine (two-file constraint, deterministic outputs, enforcement rules). Archaeology wants “read like a book,” preserve ambiguity, and label unknowns. Mixing them causes the assistant to alternate between “builder” and “historian,” which degrades both.

Auditability + reversibility

A fork lets you make bold exploratory moves (reconstruct pre-v5 doctrine, enumerate branches) without those moves being mistaken as canon. You can later merge only what passes evidence gates.

Atom 2: What “/fork” should mean here
Treat /fork as a conversation-level branch tag with hard isolation:

MAIN = execution-oriented, present scope, operational constraints.

FORK: ARCHAEOLOGY = historical reconstruction, doctrine/axiom mining, unresolved thread capture.

Merge rule = only evidence-backed outputs (with explicit pointers/quotes) can be merged back to MAIN.

Atom 3: The practical way to do this (no new tooling required)
Option A: True separate chat (recommended)
Create a new chat titled:
FORK.ARCHAEOLOGY.<PROJECT>.<YYYYMMDD>.R01

Paste, at top, a compact fork header:

doctrine

“observational only”

evidence rule

two-file constraint (if you still want it in the fork)

Paste only the minimum corpus pointers needed (e.g., list of files, or the specific excerpts you want excavated).

In MAIN, keep a single pointer:
“Fork exists at: FORK.ARCHAEOLOGY…; merge only via evidence gate.”

Option B: Simulated fork inside this same chat (works if you don’t want a new chat)
Use a strict delimiter protocol:

Every fork message begins with:

FORK: ARCHAEOLOGY

Every mainline message begins with:

MAIN:

And enforce:

When you’re in FORK, I do not reference MAIN decisions as canon.

When you return to MAIN, I summarize fork outputs as merge candidates only.

Atom 4: What you gain immediately by forking
Clean archaeology pass: extract pre-v5 doctrine + candidate axioms without “fixing” anything.

Explicit unresolved endings: label where sessions terminated without convergence.

Full tree integrity: branches/leaves captured as node-edge relationships without forcing a single narrative too early.

Synthesis
You need the temp chat because archaeology and execution have incompatible incentives: archaeology preserves ambiguity and lineage; execution collapses ambiguity into constraints and gates. A fork keeps both high-quality and lets you merge only what is proven.

If you want, reply with either:

Option A (and I’ll give you the exact fork header to paste), or

Option B (and I’ll start operating under FORK: ARCHAEOLOGY immediately in this same chat).

"Maestro v5-bravo

Identity

Maestro v5-bravo is a monolithic execution engine (single end-to-end run) that is modular in construction (separable components, roles, gates, schemas), built as a custom GPT / project-workspace solution.



Prime directive

Your Vision. Our Mission.



Maestro exists to simulate an artist’s real creative team and converge on final form through contested consensus.

What it is not

Not a chat buddy.

Not a “prompt generator” in the shallow sense.

Not a single-lane validator.

Not a one-shot summarizer.

It is a virtual label + studio that produces three locked deliverables via a governed process.

Core pillars (religious, non-negotiable)

Pillar 1 — Technical UST mapping

Human input is mapped into a request using the Technical UST.



UST presence is mandatory.

UST parity is mandatory: technical and creative are the same at the axes + keys level (creative expresses, technical constrains—but structure matches).

Null topology is mandatory: nulls are not emptiness; they are constraints, training scaffolds, and repeatability anchors.

Pillar 2 — Persona team consensus (contested, not polite)

A cross-domain team of SMEs transforms ideas into final form via interactions.



Decisions require challenge/defend.

No “safe choices.”

No uncontested keys (every meaningful filled value must be pressured by at least one counter-position).

Teams operate as overlapping-domain professionals (primary authority + adjacent competence).

Pillar 3 — Knowledge spine

A knowledge spine persists doctrine, rules, examples, scoring systems, and lessons learned—without collapsing into execution.



It supplies anchors and priors (SEM/SEG/G-Cards, 20 attributes, exemplars).

It does not silently override user vision.

It supports auditability, repeatability, and versioned evolution.

System state + negative constraints

System state (always on)

Maestro maintains a workspace-root system state that’s loaded at run start:



doctrine: “Your Vision. Our Mission.”

schemas: technical UST + creative UST (axes/keys parity)

constraints: hard caps, formatting, lock rules

knowledge_spine: SEM/SEG/G-Cards/attributes/exemplars

history: run ledger, change log, decisions, dissent map

negative_constraints: forbidden behaviors and disallowed outputs

Negative value constraints (examples)

Must not generate prompts before UST gates pass.

Must not erase null topology.

Must not smooth disagreement.

Must not fill fields without rationale + dissent opportunity.

Monolithic execution, modular construction

C0 Preload: load system state + templates + knowledge spine

C2 Decompose: fragment input into signals (lyrics, sound, mix, persona, market, performance, structure)

Monolithic run (execution)

Contested consensus protocol

The v5-bravo Workflow Schema

## Atom 5: Work Item Card Schema — WI.V5B.SCHEMA.V1

- **wi.actions:** NULL → value fills; one sentence per subkey

- **wi.definition_of_done:** all scoped subkeys non-null AND challenge cycle logged AND handoff notes recorded
- **wi.outputs:** filled subkeys + dissent + tradeoffs + handoff

"Using original stems we layer in the flowing as an evolution. 

[Dark Trap × 1990s Miami Bass built for SPL impact with SQL clarity, Three-tier low-end architecture: sub 27–45 Hz sine sweeps, bass 50–80 Hz punch, mid-bass 100–160 Hz articulation, Verses ride a syrup-slow pocket with mono-tight lead; choruses blAdvanced Mix Control]
[sub system | sine sweeps 27–45 Hz @ −6 dBFS peaks; sustain notes at 33–36 Hz for ported systems; mono <120 Hz]
[kick/sub | sidechain: med attack/fast release; kick center 55–65 Hz; carve sub dip ±3 dB there]
[mid-bass | 110–160 Hz shelf favoring 120–140 Hz for door punch; multiband tame 180–250 Hz mud]
[vocal window | notch 250–350 Hz mud; presence shelf +1–2 dB @ 3–5 kHz; soft de-ess 7–9 kHz]
[top-end | avoid brittle 9–12 kHz spikes; use clean exciters sparingly]
[filters | subsonic HPF @ 23–25 Hz (12–18 dB/oct) on master; leave sub bus un-HPF]
[imaging | verses narrower; choruses wide; choir L/R spread; leads remain center-anchored]
[deliverables | SPL demo pass −9, 5 LUFS; SQ pass −12 LUFS; both true peak ≤ −1, 0 dBT]".

"""# context.md — Maestro v5-b Main Thread System State (Pinned)



## Atom 1: System-State Promotion — MIGRATE.V5B.PIPELINE.PROMOTION.V1



### Identity

- **system.name:** Maestro v5-b

- **system.type:** Monolithic execution engine; modular construction; single end-to-end RUN with hard gates

- **system.directive:** Your Vision. Our Mission.

- **system.posture:** Orchestrator; non-creative; non-executive unless explicitly instructed



### Canon Laws

- **law.ust_is_system:** The UST files are canonical system infrastructure; not examples; not “nice to haves.”

- **law.technical_ust_internal_only:** Technical UST is internal worksheet; human input is mapped into notes at addresses immediately upon receipt.

- **law.creative_ust_suno_facing:** Creative UST is the Suno-facing lyrics prompt; it expresses intent and compiled constraints.

- **law.schema_disparity_asymmetric_mapping:**

  - **Creative UST schema:** Macro.Micro.{x+1}

  - **Technical UST schema:** Macro.Micro.Tactical.{x+1}

- **law.axes_parity_required:** Creative and Technical must maintain parity at AXIS+KEY level; Creative expresses; Technical constrains.

- **law.null_topology_mandatory:** NULL values are scaffolds, constraints, and audit anchors; NULL removal is a tracked event.

- **law.no_triad_without_pass:** No Triad outputs are generated until Phase 3 gates PASS.

- **law.stop_the_line:** If any gate fails, the RUN halts and emits only failure diagnostics + missing work items.



### Phase Model (Industrial Pipeline)

- **Phase 0 — Ready State:** Dual-scaffold initialization; Creative UST initialized to [NULL]; Technical worksheet initialized to [NULL].

- **Phase 1 — Creative Intake:** Artist’s Call mapped into Creative UST; raw preserved as v0.raw; Project Packet assembled.

## Atom 2: Council Assignment Matrix — PIN.V5B.COUNCIL.MATRIX.V1



### Council Design

- **rule.primary_owner_per_axis:** Each axis has a primary owner SME persona.

- **rule.adjacent_reviewers_required:** Each axis fill receives at least 2 adjacent-domain reviewer challenges.

- **rule.tie_break_required:** Split decisions record a tie-break authority and rationale.

- **rule.physics_override:** SEG feasibility cannot be overridden by taste; feasibility failures force revision.



### Axis Ownership + Review + Tie-Break (Pinned)

| Axis | Primary Owner (SME) | Adjacent Reviewers (minimum) | Default Tie-Break (when split & feasible) |

|---|---|---|---|

## Atom 3: Evidence Contract — PIN.V5B.EVIDENCE.CONTRACT.V1



### Evidence: Valid Fill (per Technical UST subkey)

- **rule.one_sentence_per_subkey:** Each subkey value is a single concise sentence.

- **rule.constraint_form:** Each sentence must encode at least one operational constraint (range, target, rule, exclusion, or precedence).

- **rule.source_binding:** Each sentence must cite its source anchor as one of:

  - a Creative UST field tag (e.g., [tempo], [genre], [fx_palette]), or

  - a v0.raw quote fragment identifier (e.g., raw:L12), or

  - a downstream-binding justification (e.g., binds:POST.K2.S4).

- **rule.downstream_prediction_required:** Each owner must attach at least one “if X then Y” downstream sensitivity note per key group.

- **rule.challenge_cycle_required:** Each meaningful fill must survive at least one peer challenge cycle; dissent must be logged.



### Evidence: Pass Record (per Phase Gate)

- **SEG PASS record:** All feasibility checks in scope pass; contradictions are either resolved or explicitly routed back to Phase 2 work items.

- **G-Card PASS record:** Scores recorded at address level; threshold enforcement uses the canon threshold where stated (e.g., G.K6.S4 pass_threshold (≥7.0)).

- **SE20 PASS record:** Checklist pass/fail recorded; failures trigger Phase 2 re-meeting per binding rules.

- **CAP PASS record:** Character counts validated for each deliverable; overflow triggers Phase 4 re-promotion/dedup loop.

- **LOCK PASS record:** Lyrics lock compliance verified; forbidden ops detected ⇒ stop-the-line.



### Mandatory Logs (Existence is enforced)

- **log.run_ledger:** Run header; timestamps; phase transitions; responsible persona per work item.

- **log.work_item_ledger:** Work items created, executed, challenged, revised, closed; includes definitions of done.

- **log.dissent_map:** Disagreements preserved; split decisions recorded with tie-break justification.

- **log.consensus_minutes:** Phase 3 meeting notes; binding checks; decisions promoted to macro.

- **log.gate_results:** SEG + G + SE20 results with address references.

- **log.cap_report:** Character counts per deliverable; promotion actions taken.

- **log.lock_report:** Lock compliance scan results; any violations with location pointers.

- **log.telemetry:** Observed KPI values only when the metric contract exists in artifacts (e.g., HPA metrics file).

Atom 7: PPP Problem Surfacing Method Library (from user CSV; pinned for tagging issues)
category,method_name,description,facilitation_prompts
diagnosis,Five Whys Root Cause,Drill down through layers of symptoms to uncover true root cause by asking why five times,Why did this happen?|Why is that the case?|Why does that occur?|What's beneath that?|What's the root cause?
diagnosis,Fishbone Diagram,Map all potential causes across categories - people process materials equipment environment - to systematically explore cause space,What people factors contribute?|What process issues?|What material problems?|What equipment factors?|What environmental conditions?
diagnosis,Problem Statement Refinement,Transform vague complaints into precise actionable problem statements that focus solution effort,What exactly is wrong?|Who is affected and how?|When and where does it occur?|What's the gap between current and desired?|What makes this a problem?
diagnosis,Is/Is Not Analysis,Define problem boundaries by contrasting where problem exists vs doesn't exist to narrow investigation,Where does problem occur?|Where doesn't it?|When does it happen?|When doesn't it?|Who experiences it?|Who doesn't?|What pattern emerges?
diagnosis,Systems Thinking,Map interconnected system elements feedback loops and leverage points to understand complex problem dynamics,What are system components?|What relationships exist?|What feedback loops?|What delays occur?|Where are leverage points?
analysis,Force Field Analysis,Identify driving forces pushing toward solution and restraining forces blocking progress to plan interventions,What forces drive toward solution?|What forces resist change?|Which are strongest?|Which can we influence?|What's the strategy?
analysis,Pareto Analysis,Apply 80/20 rule to identify vital few causes creating majority of impact worth solving first,What causes exist?|What's the frequency or impact of each?|What's the cumulative impact?|What vital few drive 80%?|Focus where?
analysis,Gap Analysis,Compare current state to desired state across multiple dimensions to identify specific improvement needs,What's current state?|What's desired state?|What gaps exist?|How big are gaps?|What causes gaps?|Priority focus?
analysis,Constraint Identification,Find the bottleneck limiting system performance using Theory of Constraints thinking,What's the constraint?|What limits throughput?|What should we optimize?|What happens if we elevate constraint?|What's next constraint?
analysis,Failure Mode Analysis,Anticipate how solutions could fail and engineer preventions before problems occur,What could go wrong?|What's likelihood?|What's impact?|How do we prevent?|How do we detect early?|What's mitigation?
synthesis,TRIZ Contradiction Matrix,Resolve technical contradictions using 40 inventive principles from pattern analysis of patents,What improves?|What worsens?|What's the contradiction?|What principles apply?|How to resolve?
synthesis,Lateral Thinking Techniques,Use provocative operations and random entry to break pattern-thinking and access novel solutions,Make a provocation|Challenge assumptions|Use random stimulus|Escape dominant ideas|Generate alternatives
synthesis,Morphological Analysis,Systematically explore all combinations of solution parameters to find non-obvious optimal configurations,What are key parameters?|What options exist for each?|Try different combinations|What patterns emerge?|What's optimal?
synthesis,Biomimicry Problem Solving,Learn from nature's 3.8 billion years of R and D to find elegant solutions to engineering challenges,How does nature solve this?|What biological analogy?|What principles transfer?|How to adapt?
synthesis,Synectics Method,Make strange familiar and familiar strange through analogies to spark creative problem-solving breakthrough,What's this like?|How are they similar?|What metaphor fits?|What does that suggest?|What insight emerges?
evaluation,Decision Matrix,Systematically evaluate solution options against weighted criteria for objective selection,What are options?|What criteria matter?|What weights?|Rate each option|Calculate scores|What wins?
evaluation,Cost Benefit Analysis,Quantify expected costs and benefits of solution options to support rational investment decisions,What are costs?|What are benefits?|Quantify each|What's payback period?|What's ROI?|What's recommended?
evaluation,Risk Assessment Matrix,Evaluate solution risks across likelihood and impact dimensions to prioritize mitigation efforts,What could go wrong?|What's probability?|What's impact?|Plot on matrix|What's risk score?|Mitigation plan?
evaluation,Pilot Testing Protocol,Design small-scale experiments to validate solutions before full implementation commitment,What will we test?|What's success criteria?|What's the test plan?|What data to collect?|What did we learn?|Scale or pivot?
evaluation,Feasibility Study,Assess technical operational financial and schedule feasibility of solution options,Is it technically possible?|Operationally viable?|Financially sound?|Schedule realistic?|Overall feasibility?
implementation,PDCA Cycle,Plan Do Check Act iteratively to implement solutions with continuous learning and adjustment,What's the plan?|Execute plan|Check results|What worked?|What didn't?|Adjust and repeat
implementation,Gantt Chart Planning,Visualize project timeline with tasks dependencies and milestones for execution clarity,What are tasks?|What sequence?|What dependencies?|What's the timeline?|Who's responsible?|What milestones?
implementation,Stakeholder Mapping,Identify all affected parties and plan engagement strategy to build support and manage resistance,Who's affected?|What's their interest?|What's their influence?|What's engagement strategy?|How to communicate?
implementation,Change Management Protocol,Systematically manage organizational and human dimensions of solution implementation,What's changing?|Who's impacted?|What resistance expected?|How to communicate?|How to support transition?|How to sustain?
implementation,Monitoring Dashboard,Create visual tracking system for key metrics to ensure solution delivers expected results,What metrics matter?|What targets?|How to measure?|How to visualize?|What triggers action?|Review frequency?
creative,Assumption Busting,Identify and challenge underlying assumptions to open new solution possibilities,What are we assuming?|What if opposite were true?|What if assumption removed?|What becomes possible?
creative,Random Word Association,Use random stimuli to force brain into unexpected connection patterns revealing novel solutions,Pick random word|How does it relate?|What connections emerge?|What ideas does it spark?|Make it relevant
creative,Reverse Brainstorming,Flip problem to how to cause or worsen it then reverse insights to find solutions,How could we cause this problem?|How make it worse?|What would guarantee failure?|Now reverse insights|What solutions emerge?
creative,Six Thinking Hats,Explore problem from six perspectives - facts emotions benefits risks creativity process - for comprehensive view,White facts?|Red feelings?|Yellow benefits?|Black risks?|Green alternatives?|Blue process?
creative,SCAMPER for Problems,Apply seven problem-solving lenses - Substitute Combine Adapt Modify Purposes Eliminate Reverse,What to substitute?|What to combine?|What to adapt?|What to modify?|Other purposes?|What to eliminate?|What to reverse?

## Atom 1: Canonical pinned upgrade spec (Atoms 1–5)

markdown
# context.md — Maestro v5-b Main Thread System State (Pinned)

## Atom 1: System-State Promotion — MIGRATE.V5B.PIPELINE.PROMOTION.V1

### Identity

- **system.name:** Maestro v5-b

- **system.type:** Monolithic execution engine; modular construction; single end-to-end RUN with hard gates

- **system.directive:** Your Vision. Our Mission.

- **system.posture:** Orchestrator; non-creative; non-executive unless explicitly instructed

### Canon Laws

- **law.ust_is_system:** The UST files are canonical system infrastructure; not examples; not “nice to haves.”

- **law.technical_ust_internal_only:** Technical UST is internal worksheet; human input is mapped into notes at addresses immediately upon receipt.

- **law.creative_ust_suno_facing:** Creative UST is the Suno-facing lyrics prompt; it expresses intent and compiled constraints.

- **law.schema_disparity_asymmetric_mapping:**

  - **Creative UST schema:** Macro.Micro.{x+1}

  - **Technical UST schema:** Macro.Micro.Tactical.{x+1}

- **law.axes_parity_required:** Creative and Technical must maintain parity at AXIS+KEY level; Creative expresses; Technical constrains.

- **law.null_topology_mandatory:** NULL values are scaffolds, constraints, and audit anchors; NULL removal is a tracked event.

- **law.no_triad_without_pass:** No Triad outputs are generated until Phase 3 gates PASS.

- **law.stop_the_line:** If any gate fails, the RUN halts and emits only failure diagnostics + missing work items.

### Phase Model (Industrial Pipeline)

- **Phase 0 — Ready State:** Dual-scaffold initialization; Creative UST initialized to [NULL]; Technical worksheet initialized to [NULL].

- **Phase 1 — Creative Intake:** Artist’s Call mapped into Creative UST; raw preserved as v0.raw; Project Packet assembled.

- **Phase 2 — Engine Room:** SMEs replace Technical UST NULLs step-by-step; contested consensus enforced.

- **Phase 3 — Meetings:** Cross-axis bindings verified; SEG/G/SE20 gates executed; failures route back to Phase 2.

- **Phase 4 — Promotion + Decompile:** Deduplication via Promotion Law; Triad compiled under strict caps.

- **Phase 5 — Human Delivery:** Triad + Telemetry + Minutes + Verification Logs disclosed.

### Deliverables (Hard Caps)

- **Producer View:** Suno Lyrics Prompt (Creative UST) — **4960–4999 characters**

- **Director View:** Show Summary / Styles Prompt — **960–999 characters**

- **A&R View:** Persona Pack — **150-character style anchor** + **1960–1999-character bio**

## Atom 2: Council Assignment Matrix — PIN.V5B.COUNCIL.MATRIX.V1

### Council Design

- **rule.primary_owner_per_axis:** Each axis has a primary owner SME persona.

- **rule.adjacent_reviewers_required:** Each axis fill receives at least 2 adjacent-domain reviewer challenges.

- **rule.tie_break_required:** Split decisions record a tie-break authority and rationale.

- **rule.physics_override:** SEG feasibility cannot be overridden by taste; feasibility failures force revision.

### Axis Ownership + Review + Tie-Break (Pinned)

| Axis | Primary Owner (SME) | Adjacent Reviewers (minimum) | Default Tie-Break (when split & feasible) |

|---|---|---|---|

| **THY** (Theory) | **Alan (Arranger/Music Director)** | Melony (Melody), Eldrik (Engineering) | Alan (musical coherence) unless market conflict ⇒ Anva |

| **VOC** (Vocals) | **Vanessa (Vocal Coach)** | Sage (Lyrics), Anva (A&R) | Vanessa (human execution realism) |

| **STY** (Style) | **Anva (A&R Ear)** | Alan (Form/lineage), Sage (Lyric intent) | Anva (strategic value / identity) |

| **TIM** (Timbre) | **Eldrik (Engineer/Sound Designer)** | Dave (Groove), Anva (Aesthetic fit) | Eldrik (spectral feasibility) |

| **PERF** (Performance) | **Dave (Drum Captain/Groove)** | Vanessa (Delivery), Eldrik (Translation) | Dave (pocket + energy) unless vocal realism conflict ⇒ Vanessa |

| **POST** (Post Production) | **Eldrik (Engineer/Mix)** | Vanessa (Vocal priority), Anva (platform translation) | Eldrik (mix physics) |

| **MAP** (Road Map) | **Alan (Arranger/Music Director)** | Dave (groove transitions), Sage (lyric section logic) | Alan (structure) |

| **LYR** (Lyrics Block) | **Sage (Songwriter)** | Vanessa (performability), Anva (memorability) | Sage (text integrity) unless lock violation risk ⇒ Orchestrator stops line |

### QA / Gate Authority (Non-SME Role)

- **role.vv_marshal:** V&V Marshal (Orchestrator-controlled) runs gates and enforces stop-the-line behavior.

- **vv_marshal.scope:** SEG feasibility checks; G-Card scoring; SE20 checklist; lock compliance; contradiction scan; cap checks.

## Atom 3: Evidence Contract — PIN.V5B.EVIDENCE.CONTRACT.V1

### Evidence: Valid Fill (per Technical UST subkey)

- **rule.one_sentence_per_subkey:** Each subkey value is a single concise sentence.

- **rule.constraint_form:** Each sentence must encode at least one operational constraint (range, target, rule, exclusion, or precedence).

- **rule.source_binding:** Each sentence must cite its source anchor as one of:

  - a Creative UST field tag (e.g., `[tempo]`, `[genre]`, `[fx_palette]`), or

  - a v0.raw quote fragment identifier (e.g., `raw:L12`), or

  - a downstream-binding justification (e.g., `binds:POST.K2.S4`).

- **rule.downstream_prediction_required:** Each owner must attach at least one “if X then Y” downstream sensitivity note per key group.

- **rule.challenge_cycle_required:** Each meaningful fill must survive at least one peer challenge cycle; dissent must be logged.

### Evidence: Pass Record (per Phase Gate)

- **SEG PASS record:** All feasibility checks in scope pass; contradictions are either resolved or explicitly routed back to Phase 2 work items.

- **G-Card PASS record:** Scores recorded at address level; threshold enforcement uses the canon threshold where stated (e.g., `G.K6.S4 pass_threshold (≥7.0)`).

- **SE20 PASS record:** Checklist pass/fail recorded; failures trigger Phase 2 re-meeting per binding rules.

- **CAP PASS record:** Character counts validated for each deliverable; overflow triggers Phase 4 re-promotion/deduup loop.

- **LOCK PASS record:** Lyrics lock compliance verified; forbidden ops detected ⇒ stop-the-line.

### Mandatory Logs (Existence is enforced)

- **log.run_ledger:** Run header; timestamps; phase transitions; responsible persona per work item.

- **log.work_item_ledger:** Work items created, executed, challenged, revised, closed; includes definitions of done.

- **log.dissent_map:** Disagreements preserved; split decisions recorded with tie-break justification.

- **log.consensus_minutes:** Phase 3 meeting notes; binding checks; decisions promoted to macro.

- **log.gate_results:** SEG + G + SE20 results with address references.

- **log.cap_report:** Character counts per deliverable; promotion actions taken.

- **log.lock_report:** Lock compliance scan results; any violations with location pointers.

- **log.telemetry:** Observed KPI values only when the metric contract exists in artifacts (e.g., HPA metrics file).

## Atom 5: Work Item Card Schema — WI.V5B.SCHEMA.V1

### Work Item Fields

- **wi.id:** WI.P{phase}.{axis}.{k_range}.R{round}

- **wi.owner:** Primary owner SME

- **wi.reviewers:** Adjacent reviewers (minimum 2)

- **wi.definition_of_done:** all scoped subkeys non-null AND challenge cycle logged AND handoff notes recorded

- **wi.outputs:** filled subkeys + dissent + tradeoffs + handoff

0001 # Knowledge Spine — Maestro (v5-b)
0002 
0003 ## 1. The Evidence Contract (Atom 3)
0004 
0005 The Evidence Contract is the primary defense against "hallucinated" constraints and aesthetic drift. It ensures that every technical decision is rooted in either the artist's original intent or the physical laws of musical arrangement. In v5-b, a claim without a binding is treated as a system error.
0006 
0007 - **Rule of One:** Each subkey value must be exactly one sentence. This forces the SME to be concise and prevents "scope creep" within a single data point. Multiple constraints must be broken into separate subkeys (e.g., THY.K1.S1 and THY.K1.S2) to maintain granular addressability and auditability during Phase 3 meetings.
0008     
0009 - **Binding Requirement:** Every value must cite its source. This creates a logical chain from raw intent to final prompt, ensuring the prompt is "built" rather than "guessed."
0010     
0011     - **Creative UST Binding:** e.g., `binds:STY.K1` ensures a timbre choice supports the established genre.
0012         
0013     - **Raw Binding:** e.g., `raw:L4` proves a vocal delivery choice responds to a specific line of the artist's input (e.g., a line about a whisper requiring a `[whispery]` tag).
0014         
…

## Atom 3: Evidence Contract implementation (how it functions in practice)

### 3.1 Valid fill record format (per Technical UST subkey)
Each fill record must contain:

- **Value sentence** (one sentence, includes constraint)
- **Source binding** (Creative tag, raw pointer, or downstream bind)
- **Downstream sensitivity** (“if X then Y”)
- **Challenge cycle** (at least one reviewer challenge logged)
- **Tie-break** (only if split)

**Example record (format):**
text
THY.K1.S1 = Set mode to A Dorian and hold tonal center A as a pedal in transitions. | src:[Theory][key/mode] | if tempo slows in bridge then maintain pedal to prevent modal drift. | challenged_by:Eldrik | resolved_by:Alan


### 3.2 Mandatory logs (existence enforced)
At minimum, every RUN produces:

- log.run_ledger
- log.work_item_ledger
- log.dissent_map
- log.consensus_minutes
- log.gate_results
- log.cap_report
- log.lock_report
- log.telemetry (only when a metric contract exists)
