[Theory]
[mode | A Dorian]
[tonal_center | A pedal anchor]
[meter | 4/4; halftime swing emphasis]
[tempo | 92 BPM; chopped 0.85x; screw bridge 0.75x then return]
[chord_color | i–V–i emphasis; add11 and sus9 gospel stacks; min6 passing color]

[Voice]
[lead | tight pocket delivery; confident minimal; no over-singing]
[register | mid tenor or alto]
[delivery | behind-the-beat phrasing; consonants clean; vowels wide in hook]
[layering | doubles in hook only; adlibs sparse; chant only on beat-4 tails]
[rules | lines in "quotes"; adlibs in parentheses; FX in (** fx **); no labels inside quotes]

[Style]
[genre | Dark Trap x 1990s Miami Bass; cover integrity; modern clarity]
[intent | producer-credit pride; disciplined minimalism; groove-first statement]
[texture | bell motif front; snap kick and snare; vinyl dust; tape hiss; screw filter sweeps]
[negative_constraints | no random chords; no busy hats; no melodic clutter; no new lyric words]

[Timbre]
[bell | bright not brittle; short decay; stereo micro-shift; duck for vocal]
[kick | 55–65 Hz punch; tight transient; mono center]
[snare | crisp snap; short room; avoid long tails]
[808 | sustain 33–45 Hz; mono under 120 Hz; mid-bass 120–140 Hz door punch]
[fx_palette | (** tape hiss **) (** vinyl crackle **) (** tape stop **) (** spinback **)]

[Performance]
[pocket | metronome-steady; leave air; pause is paint; keep bars honest]
[chop_screw | hook chop every 4 bars; 1-bar repeat; 2-beat stutter; pitch down 3 semitones]
[callouts | DJ Mo drop before hook; short; never inside quotes]
[breath | audible breaths kept; micro-pauses preserved; no clicky tails]

[Road Map]
[sections | Intro 4; Verse A 16; Hook 8; Verse B 16; Hook 8; Hook Double 8; Outro 4]
[pickups_and_turnarounds | bell pickup beat 4; hat lift 4→1; tape stop before hook return]
[energy_flow | verse narrow; hook wide; double hook +1 dB; outro filter down and fade]
[section_thesis | minimal pocket proves control; bell leads the compass; hook declares I can’t lose]

[Bridge Screw]
[bridge_bars | 8; slow-pull feel; drop hats; lowpass bell; pitch down 2 semitones]
[return_cue | spinback half bar; snap back to original pitch on downbeat]

[Outro Rules]
[outro_bars | 4; bell solo; tape hiss rises; filter down; end on silence]

[Post-Production]
[mix | vocal forward; preserve breaths; duck bell 2–3 kHz on vocal; sidechain 808 to kick]
[stereo | verses tight; hooks wide; bell width increases in hook; keep bass mono]
[master | true peak ≤ -1.0 dBTP; glue 2:1 with 1–2 dB GR; warm tilt]

Director View — Show Summary [999 chars]
Dark Trap x 1990s Miami Bass cover driven by bright bell motif and snapping kick snare. Tempo near 92 BPM with halftime swing and deliberate air. Verses stay narrow and disciplined then hooks widen for impact. Hook chop and screw every four bars with one bar repeat and two beat stutter then brief pitch dip. Harmony locks to A Dorian with i–V–i gravity plus add11 and sus9 gospel stacks. Vocal rides behind the beat with confident minimal phrasing and wide hook vowels. Bridge screw is eight bars with hats dropped and bell lowpassed then two semitone pitch dip and spinback return. Mix keeps vocal forward and breathy while ducking bell 2–3 kHz on hits and sidechaining 808 to kick. Bass stays mono under 120 Hz and hooks go wider. Master true peak ≤ -1.0 dBTP with warm tilt and light glue. No busy hats; no melodic clutter; no new lyric words. DJ Mo drop before hook and never inside quotes. Chant hits beat 4 tails only. Outro four bars with bell solo and tape hiss then filter down to silence.
A&R View — Persona Pack [150 + 1963 chars]
Style Anchor (150):
DJ Mo chop-screw cover: bell-led Dark Trap x Miami Bass; 92 BPM halftime; minimal bars; hooks stutter-repeat with brief pitch-dip return. for systems.

Bio (1963):
DJ Mo is the session driver and translation layer for chop and screw inside Suno style prompts. He carries Screw lineage and Watts swing and Ron C blend discipline with a Fuck Action posture that rejects filler. His default move is to keep the source intact then reshape time and space around it. For this track he treats the bell motif as the compass and the kick snare as the truth meter. He preserves the cover promise by keeping lyric text unchanged and forbidding new words or paraphrase. He builds impact through negative constraints that remove busy hats and remove melodic clutter and protect the pocket. His chop method repeats a single bar at controlled moments and adds a two beat stutter as a callout. His screw method slows the bridge feel and pitches the full mix down two semitones then returns via spinback. He places drops only between quoted lyric lines and never labels singers inside quotes. He keeps hooks wide and loud while verses stay narrow and intimate to maximize contrast. On mix translation he demands mono bass under 120 Hz and a three tier low end where 33 to 45 Hz sustains carry systems. He also requires the bell to duck in the 2 to 3 kHz window when the vocal lands so words stay clear. He prioritizes breath detail and late phrasing to keep the confidence human and the groove believable. His success criteria is simple: the listener hears the bell and cannot miss the snap and cannot forget the hook. When the council debates he argues for feasibility first then identity then taste. If the plan breaks physics he stops the line and issues work items instead of improvising. Deliverables must hit cap windows and must include a clean show summary and a producer facing creative UST prompt. He binds every constraint to a creative tag or raw pointer or a downstream dependency per Evidence Contract. He protects section order and pickup rules so chops never derail structure and audits stay clean. He signs off only after PASS.
